People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Yeah… there was a time like that, I think. A few weeks where I wasn’t so sunk down. I was eating a little more regular, getting through the route at work without standing there staring at the mop bucket forever.

Yeah… I think so. It wasn’t just a day or two. It was, I don’t know, several weeks maybe, where I wasn’t in that deep hole like before.

Yeah… yeah, I did. Things just kind of went flat.

I used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, even if I didn’t catch much. Just sitting there with the line out, coffee in the thermos… there’s a spot where the weeds always snag if you cast too far left, and I knew that, I liked knowing that. But during those low times I’d just let the gear sit.

Same with crosswords in the morning. I used to do them with coffee, pencil all chewed up, you know. Or at work, showing the younger guys little tricks for stripping a floor so they don’t gouge it up. I used to get something out of that.

But lately… or during those times, I mostly just let those things sit. They don’t really give me anything back.

Like he’s standing over by the stairwell. And I’ll turn and look, because it feels that close, but… there’s nobody there.\n\nDoctor: When the voice happens, do you feel depressed at those times?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. I mean, not every single time I hear it, maybe, but when I was really at the bottom—like barely eating, just feeling guilty about everything, even stupid things like not getting the trash liners folded right—I was hearing that man’s voice at work too.\n\nThat was also when I started feeling like people in the building were watching me. Like from the offices, or cameras maybe… I know buildings have cameras, of course, I clean around them all the time, little black domes in the corners. But it felt more personal than that. And I was having thoughts like… maybe I wouldn’t care if I didn’t wake up. So, yeah, the voice was happening when I was depressed like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard that voice during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a time like that, I think. A few weeks where I wasn’t so sunk down. I was eating a little more regular, getting through the route at work without standing there staring at the mop bucket forever.\n\nBut even then, I’d be in the stairwell and hear my name. Just my name, like someone calling from around the corner. And I still had it in my head that the hallway cameras weren’t just there for security… like they were being used to keep track of me specifically.\n\nThose cameras, they put them in those little smoked domes, you know, and you can’t tell which way they’re pointing. I used to clean under them and try not to look up too much. But yeah… that was when I wasn’t feeling as depressed as before.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least 2 weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt the cameras were tracking you, while you were not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It wasn’t just a day or two. It was, I don’t know, several weeks maybe, where I wasn’t in that deep hole like before.\n\nI was eating meals more regular, getting through work a little better… still slow, but not just standing there useless. But I’d still hear my name in the stairwell, clear enough that I’d stop and look.\n\nAnd the cameras… those hallway cameras, with the dark little domes, I still felt like they were tracking me in particular. Not just normal security. I know that sounds… but yeah, that went on while I wasn’t feeling so depressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic or high, much more than normal?\n\nPatient: Unusually energetic or high… I don’t know. I don’t think I get “high” like that, not like bouncing off the walls or anything.\n\nSometimes if I get a decent night’s sleep, I can move a little faster at work, you know, get the trash pulled before the offices start filling up. But that’s not… I mean, that’s just having a better day. The floors take forever when people track in salt in the winter, and then you’re going over the same spot again and again—\n\nSorry. I guess I’m not sure what you mean exactly. I haven’t really noticed a time where I felt way more energetic than normal.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you lose interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I did. Things just kind of went flat.\n\nI used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, even if I didn’t catch much. Just sitting there with the line out, coffee in the thermos… there’s a spot where the weeds always snag if you cast too far left, and I knew that, I liked knowing that. But during those low times I’d just let the gear sit.\n\nSame with crosswords in the morning. I used to do them with coffee, pencil all chewed up, you know. Or at work, showing the younger guys little tricks for stripping a floor so they don’t gouge it up. I used to get something out of that.\n\nBut lately… or during those times, I mostly just let those things sit. They don’t really give me anything back.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Yeah… yes. It wasn’t just a rough weekend or something.

The low mood has been there most of the day, pretty much nearly every day, for… about a year and a couple months now, I think. It started not too long after I moved. And I still had it in my head that the hallway cameras weren’t just there for security… like they were being used to keep track of me specifically.\n\nThose cameras, they put them in those little smoked domes, you know, and you can’t tell which way they’re pointing. I used to clean under them and try not to look up too much. But yeah… that was when I wasn’t feeling as depressed as before.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least 2 weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt the cameras were tracking you, while you were not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It wasn’t just a day or two. It was, I don’t know, several weeks maybe, where I wasn’t in that deep hole like before.\n\nI was eating meals more regular, getting through work a little better… still slow, but not just standing there useless. But I’d still hear my name in the stairwell, clear enough that I’d stop and look.\n\nAnd the cameras… those hallway cameras, with the dark little domes, I still felt like they were tracking me in particular. Not just normal security. I know that sounds… but yeah, that went on while I wasn’t feeling so depressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic or high, much more than normal?\n\nPatient: Unusually energetic or high… I don’t know. I don’t think I get “high” like that, not like bouncing off the walls or anything.\n\nSometimes if I get a decent night’s sleep, I can move a little faster at work, you know, get the trash pulled before the offices start filling up. But that’s not… I mean, that’s just having a better day. The floors take forever when people track in salt in the winter, and then you’re going over the same spot again and again—\n\nSorry. I guess I’m not sure what you mean exactly. I haven’t really noticed a time where I felt way more energetic than normal.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you lose interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I did. Things just kind of went flat.\n\nI used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, even if I didn’t catch much. Just sitting there with the line out, coffee in the thermos… there’s a spot where the weeds always snag if you cast too far left, and I knew that, I liked knowing that. But during those low times I’d just let the gear sit.\n\nSame with crosswords in the morning. I used to do them with coffee, pencil all chewed up, you know. Or at work, showing the younger guys little tricks for stripping a floor so they don’t gouge it up. I used to get something out of that.\n\nBut lately… or during those times, I mostly just let those things sit. They don’t really give me anything back.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your depressed mood lasted most of the day, nearly every day, for at least 2 weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. It wasn’t just a rough weekend or something.\n\nThe low mood has been there most of the day, pretty much nearly every day, for… about a year and a couple months now, I think. It started not too long after I moved. I remember because I still had boxes stacked in the corner, and I kept telling myself I’d unpack them once I felt settled, but then weeks just kept going by.\n\nSo yeah, definitely more than two weeks. Yeah… there was a stretch like that, I think.

It wasn’t like I was cheerful exactly, but my mood had lifted some. I was getting through meals a little better, and work didn’t feel quite as impossible. And I still had it in my head that the hallway cameras weren’t just there for security… like they were being used to keep track of me specifically.\n\nThose cameras, they put them in those little smoked domes, you know, and you can’t tell which way they’re pointing. I used to clean under them and try not to look up too much. But yeah… that was when I wasn’t feeling as depressed as before.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least 2 weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt the cameras were tracking you, while you were not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It wasn’t just a day or two. It was, I don’t know, several weeks maybe, where I wasn’t in that deep hole like before.\n\nI was eating meals more regular, getting through work a little better… still slow, but not just standing there useless. But I’d still hear my name in the stairwell, clear enough that I’d stop and look.\n\nAnd the cameras… those hallway cameras, with the dark little domes, I still felt like they were tracking me in particular. Not just normal security. I know that sounds… but yeah, that went on while I wasn’t feeling so depressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic or high, much more than normal?\n\nPatient: Unusually energetic or high… I don’t know. I don’t think I get “high” like that, not like bouncing off the walls or anything.\n\nSometimes if I get a decent night’s sleep, I can move a little faster at work, you know, get the trash pulled before the offices start filling up. But that’s not… I mean, that’s just having a better day. The floors take forever when people track in salt in the winter, and then you’re going over the same spot again and again—\n\nSorry. I guess I’m not sure what you mean exactly. I haven’t really noticed a time where I felt way more energetic than normal.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you lose interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I did. Things just kind of went flat.\n\nI used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, even if I didn’t catch much. Just sitting there with the line out, coffee in the thermos… there’s a spot where the weeds always snag if you cast too far left, and I knew that, I liked knowing that. But during those low times I’d just let the gear sit.\n\nSame with crosswords in the morning. I used to do them with coffee, pencil all chewed up, you know. Or at work, showing the younger guys little tricks for stripping a floor so they don’t gouge it up. I used to get something out of that.\n\nBut lately… or during those times, I mostly just let those things sit. They don’t really give me anything back.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your depressed mood lasted most of the day, nearly every day, for at least 2 weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. It wasn’t just a rough weekend or something.\n\nThe low mood has been there most of the day, pretty much nearly every day, for… about a year and a couple months now, I think. It started not too long after I moved. I remember because I still had boxes stacked in the corner, and I kept telling myself I’d unpack them once I felt settled, but then weeks just kept going by.\n\nSo yeah, definitely more than two weeks. It’s been around a year and two months, give or take.\n\nDoctor: During the time you were hearing the voice or felt the cameras were tracking you, was there ever a period when those experiences were happening but you were not feeling depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a stretch like that, I think.\n\nIt wasn’t like I was cheerful exactly, but my mood had lifted some. I was getting through meals a little better, and work didn’t feel quite as impossible. I could get through my shift, do the stairwells, the hallway floors… you know, those cameras are in every hallway now, and some of them have that little red light—well, I guess not all of them do.\n\nBut even then, when I wasn’t feeling so depressed, I still heard my name in the stairwell. And I still felt sure the hallway cameras were being used to track me. So… yeah, it wasn’t only when I was really low.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, not every single time I hear it, maybe, but when I was really at the bottom—like barely eating, just feeling guilty about everything, even stupid things like not getting the trash liners folded right—I was hearing that man’s voice at work too.\n\nThat was also when I started feeling like people in the building were watching me. Like from the offices, or cameras maybe… I know buildings have cameras, of course, I clean around them all the time, little black domes in the corners. But it felt more personal than that. And I was having thoughts like… maybe I wouldn’t care if I didn’t wake up. So, yeah, the voice was happening when I was depressed like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard that voice during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a time like that, I think. A few weeks where I wasn’t so sunk down. I was eating a little more regular, getting through the route at work without standing there staring at the mop bucket forever.\n\nBut even then, I’d be in the stairwell and hear my name. Just my name, like someone calling from around the corner. And I still had it in my head that the hallway cameras weren’t just there for security… like they were being used to keep track of me specifically.\n\nThose cameras, they put them in those little smoked domes, you know, and you can’t tell which way they’re pointing. I used to clean under them and try not to look up too much. But yeah… that was when I wasn’t feeling as depressed as before.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least 2 weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt the cameras were tracking you, while you were not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It wasn’t just a day or two. It was, I don’t know, several weeks maybe, where I wasn’t in that deep hole like before.\n\nI was eating meals more regular, getting through work a little better… still slow, but not just standing there useless. But I’d still hear my name in the stairwell, clear enough that I’d stop and look.\n\nAnd the cameras… those hallway cameras, with the dark little domes, I still felt like they were tracking me in particular. Not just normal security. I know that sounds… but yeah, that went on while I wasn’t feeling so depressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic or high, much more than normal?\n\nPatient: Unusually energetic or high… I don’t know. I don’t think I get “high” like that, not like bouncing off the walls or anything.\n\nSometimes if I get a decent night’s sleep, I can move a little faster at work, you know, get the trash pulled before the offices start filling up. But that’s not… I mean, that’s just having a better day. The floors take forever when people track in salt in the winter, and then you’re going over the same spot again and again—\n\nSorry. I guess I’m not sure what you mean exactly. I haven’t really noticed a time where I felt way more energetic than normal.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you lose interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I did. Things just kind of went flat.\n\nI used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, even if I didn’t catch much. Just sitting there with the line out, coffee in the thermos… there’s a spot where the weeds always snag if you cast too far left, and I knew that, I liked knowing that. But during those low times I’d just let the gear sit.\n\nSame with crosswords in the morning. I used to do them with coffee, pencil all chewed up, you know. Or at work, showing the younger guys little tricks for stripping a floor so they don’t gouge it up. I used to get something out of that.\n\nBut lately… or during those times, I mostly just let those things sit. They don’t really give me anything back.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your depressed mood lasted most of the day, nearly every day, for at least 2 weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. It wasn’t just a rough weekend or something.\n\nThe low mood has been there most of the day, pretty much nearly every day, for… about a year and a couple months now, I think. It started not too long after I moved. I remember because I still had boxes stacked in the corner, and I kept telling myself I’d unpack them once I felt settled, but then weeks just kept going by.\n\nSo yeah, definitely more than two weeks. It’s been around a year and two months, give or take.\n\nDoctor: During the time you were hearing the voice or felt the cameras were tracking you, was there ever a period when those experiences were happening but you were not feeling depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a stretch like that, I think.\n\nIt wasn’t like I was cheerful exactly, but my mood had lifted some. I was getting through meals a little better, and work didn’t feel quite as impossible. I could get through my shift, do the stairwells, the hallway floors… you know, those cameras are in every hallway now, and some of them have that little red light—well, I guess not all of them do.\n\nBut even then, when I wasn’t feeling so depressed, I still heard my name in the stairwell. And I still felt sure the hallway cameras were being used to track me. So… yeah, it wasn’t only when I was really low.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling that the cameras were tracking you been going on for at least 6 months continuously?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say so. It’s been about 11 months now, the voice and that feeling with the cameras.\n\nI remember because I kept thinking it was just the building, you know, the way sound carries in stairwells. Those stairwells echo funny after they wax the floors, and the cameras—well, they’re everywhere now, even in grocery stores. But… no, it’s been going on pretty steady for around 11 months.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Completely free? No… I don’t think I’d say completely.

There were several weeks where my mood had lifted some. Like, I was eating a little better, and getting through work didn’t feel quite so impossible. I could do the hallways and the stairwells without dragging myself as much… though the stairwells are where I’d still hear my name sometimes.

And the cameras, too — I still felt sure they were tracking me in the hallways, even during that better stretch. So I wasn’t as depressed then, but I wouldn’t say I was totally free of it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say not depressed at all. There were several weeks where I was… better, I guess. Like I could eat more regular, and getting through work wasn’t such a mountain. I’d do the hallways, mop around the stairwells, and I wasn’t dragging as bad.

But the voice was still there sometimes — hearing my name in the stairwell. And I still felt like the hallway cameras were tracking me. Those cameras are everywhere now, and some of them are aimed at odd angles, like not where you’d expect… but yeah, even when my mood had lifted, that part didn’t really stop.

Like he’s standing over by the stairwell. And I still had it in my head that the hallway cameras weren’t just there for security… like they were being used to keep track of me specifically.\n\nThose cameras, they put them in those little smoked domes, you know, and you can’t tell which way they’re pointing. I used to clean under them and try not to look up too much. But yeah… that was when I wasn’t feeling as depressed as before.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least 2 weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt the cameras were tracking you, while you were not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It wasn’t just a day or two. It was, I don’t know, several weeks maybe, where I wasn’t in that deep hole like before.\n\nI was eating meals more regular, getting through work a little better… still slow, but not just standing there useless. But I’d still hear my name in the stairwell, clear enough that I’d stop and look.\n\nAnd the cameras… those hallway cameras, with the dark little domes, I still felt like they were tracking me in particular. Not just normal security. I know that sounds… but yeah, that went on while I wasn’t feeling so depressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic or high, much more than normal?\n\nPatient: Unusually energetic or high… I don’t know. I don’t think I get “high” like that, not like bouncing off the walls or anything.\n\nSometimes if I get a decent night’s sleep, I can move a little faster at work, you know, get the trash pulled before the offices start filling up. But that’s not… I mean, that’s just having a better day. The floors take forever when people track in salt in the winter, and then you’re going over the same spot again and again—\n\nSorry. I guess I’m not sure what you mean exactly. I haven’t really noticed a time where I felt way more energetic than normal.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you lose interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I did. Things just kind of went flat.\n\nI used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, even if I didn’t catch much. Just sitting there with the line out, coffee in the thermos… there’s a spot where the weeds always snag if you cast too far left, and I knew that, I liked knowing that. But during those low times I’d just let the gear sit.\n\nSame with crosswords in the morning. I used to do them with coffee, pencil all chewed up, you know. Or at work, showing the younger guys little tricks for stripping a floor so they don’t gouge it up. I used to get something out of that.\n\nBut lately… or during those times, I mostly just let those things sit. They don’t really give me anything back.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your depressed mood lasted most of the day, nearly every day, for at least 2 weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. It wasn’t just a rough weekend or something.\n\nThe low mood has been there most of the day, pretty much nearly every day, for… about a year and a couple months now, I think. It started not too long after I moved. I remember because I still had boxes stacked in the corner, and I kept telling myself I’d unpack them once I felt settled, but then weeks just kept going by.\n\nSo yeah, definitely more than two weeks. It’s been around a year and two months, give or take.\n\nDoctor: During the time you were hearing the voice or felt the cameras were tracking you, was there ever a period when those experiences were happening but you were not feeling depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a stretch like that, I think.\n\nIt wasn’t like I was cheerful exactly, but my mood had lifted some. I was getting through meals a little better, and work didn’t feel quite as impossible. I could get through my shift, do the stairwells, the hallway floors… you know, those cameras are in every hallway now, and some of them have that little red light—well, I guess not all of them do.\n\nBut even then, when I wasn’t feeling so depressed, I still heard my name in the stairwell. And I still felt sure the hallway cameras were being used to track me. So… yeah, it wasn’t only when I was really low.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling that the cameras were tracking you been going on for at least 6 months continuously?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say so. It’s been about 11 months now, the voice and that feeling with the cameras.\n\nI remember because I kept thinking it was just the building, you know, the way sound carries in stairwells. Those stairwells echo funny after they wax the floors, and the cameras—well, they’re everywhere now, even in grocery stores. But… no, it’s been going on pretty steady for around 11 months.\n\nDoctor: During that stretch when you still heard the voice and felt tracked by the cameras, were you free of depression completely?\n\nPatient: Completely free? No… I don’t think I’d say completely.\n\nThere were several weeks where my mood had lifted some. Like, I was eating a little better, and getting through work didn’t feel quite so impossible. I could do the hallways and the stairwells without dragging myself as much… though the stairwells are where I’d still hear my name sometimes.\n\nAnd the cameras, too — I still felt sure they were tracking me in the hallways, even during that better stretch. So I wasn’t as depressed then, but I wouldn’t say I was totally free of it.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time lasting at least 2 weeks when you heard the voice or felt tracked by the cameras while you were not depressed at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say not depressed at all. There were several weeks where I was… better, I guess. Like I could eat more regular, and getting through work wasn’t such a mountain. I’d do the hallways, mop around the stairwells, and I wasn’t dragging as bad.\n\nBut the voice was still there sometimes — hearing my name in the stairwell. And I still felt like the hallway cameras were tracking me. Those cameras are everywhere now, and some of them are aimed at odd angles, like not where you’d expect… but yeah, even when my mood had lifted, that part didn’t really stop.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, were you sleeping much less or much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I didn’t keep track of it like that.\n\nSome nights felt different than others, but with the cleaning work, the hours and the building routine kind of blur together. You know, you finish one floor and then another, and by the time you get home it’s hard to tell what’s “usual” anymore. So… I can’t say for sure if it was much less or much more.\n\nDoctor: At any time since the voice and the camera-tracking feeling started, were you completely free of depression for at least 2 weeks while those experiences were still happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I was completely free of it, no. There was a stretch of several weeks where I was doing better — I was eating more normal, getting through my work a little easier, not feeling so weighed down every minute.\n\nBut the voice was still happening, like hearing my name in the stairwell, and I still believed the hallway cameras were tracking me. Those hallways have cameras in such strange spots, you know, and when you’re cleaning at night you notice all that. So… better mood, yes, but not totally free of depression.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you feel down or depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, most of the time. Since the move, it’s like that sadness and low energy were just kind of sitting there in the background. Even on days when I was more caught up worrying about the cameras or hearing things, the down feeling was still there underneath.\n\nI’d be doing the cleaning, taking out trash, checking the same hallway lights, and it’s funny what you notice in buildings after hours… but yeah, the guilt and not really enjoying much of anything, that was there a lot. Nearly every day, I’d say, or close to it.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, was your appetite decreased?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it was decreased. Most days food just didn’t seem like food, more like cardboard or paper. I’d get home after the shift and maybe eat a few crackers, or just skip it because it felt like too much trouble.\n\nAnd then I’d notice my work pants hanging looser, which—those pants are already cheap and the waistband never sits right, the kind they give you with those scratchy seams—but yeah, looser than they used to be. So I think I wasn’t eating much.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, sure, but whether it was most of the day, nearly every day, for two whole weeks… I don’t know. Time gets blurry when you look back like that.

There were times years ago where I felt low, but I was still going to work and doing what I had to do. Then again, work can cover things up — you mop a floor, empty bins, keep moving, and the day goes by. Some buildings have those old buzzing lights, and that kind of thing can get under your skin after a while…

But, uh, before this past year? I can’t say for sure that there was another period like that. I don’t really know.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess what brought me in is I don’t really feel like myself anymore. I’m just kind of… flat, you know? I don’t talk to people much, even when they’re right there. At work, the cleaning route—something I used to just do—it feels like I’m pushing a loaded cart uphill just to get started.\n\nAnd the building has these long hallways, funny how you notice the floor wax smell more when it’s quiet… but, yeah, mostly I’ve just gone quiet. Like I’m there, but not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. Mostly when I’m mopping after hours, when the place is empty and the lights make that buzzing sound—you know, those old hallway lights, they never fix them right.\n\nI’ll hear a man’s voice say my name, clear as anything, or say I missed a spot. Like he’s standing over by the stairwell. And I’ll turn and look, because it feels that close, but… there’s nobody there.\n\nDoctor: When the voice happens, do you feel depressed at those times?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. I mean, not every single time I hear it, maybe, but when I was really at the bottom—like barely eating, just feeling guilty about everything, even stupid things like not getting the trash liners folded right—I was hearing that man’s voice at work too.\n\nThat was also when I started feeling like people in the building were watching me. Like from the offices, or cameras maybe… I know buildings have cameras, of course, I clean around them all the time, little black domes in the corners. But it felt more personal than that. And I was having thoughts like… maybe I wouldn’t care if I didn’t wake up. So, yeah, the voice was happening when I was depressed like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard that voice during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a time like that, I think. A few weeks where I wasn’t so sunk down. I was eating a little more regular, getting through the route at work without standing there staring at the mop bucket forever.\n\nBut even then, I’d be in the stairwell and hear my name. Just my name, like someone calling from around the corner. And I still had it in my head that the hallway cameras weren’t just there for security… like they were being used to keep track of me specifically.\n\nThose cameras, they put them in those little smoked domes, you know, and you can’t tell which way they’re pointing. I used to clean under them and try not to look up too much. But yeah… that was when I wasn’t feeling as depressed as before.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least 2 weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt the cameras were tracking you, while you were not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It wasn’t just a day or two. It was, I don’t know, several weeks maybe, where I wasn’t in that deep hole like before.\n\nI was eating meals more regular, getting through work a little better… still slow, but not just standing there useless. But I’d still hear my name in the stairwell, clear enough that I’d stop and look.\n\nAnd the cameras… those hallway cameras, with the dark little domes, I still felt like they were tracking me in particular. Not just normal security. I know that sounds… but yeah, that went on while I wasn’t feeling so depressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic or high, much more than normal?\n\nPatient: Unusually energetic or high… I don’t know. I don’t think I get “high” like that, not like bouncing off the walls or anything.\n\nSometimes if I get a decent night’s sleep, I can move a little faster at work, you know, get the trash pulled before the offices start filling up. But that’s not… I mean, that’s just having a better day. The floors take forever when people track in salt in the winter, and then you’re going over the same spot again and again—\n\nSorry. I guess I’m not sure what you mean exactly. I haven’t really noticed a time where I felt way more energetic than normal.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you lose interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I did. Things just kind of went flat.\n\nI used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, even if I didn’t catch much. Just sitting there with the line out, coffee in the thermos… there’s a spot where the weeds always snag if you cast too far left, and I knew that, I liked knowing that. But during those low times I’d just let the gear sit.\n\nSame with crosswords in the morning. I used to do them with coffee, pencil all chewed up, you know. Or at work, showing the younger guys little tricks for stripping a floor so they don’t gouge it up. I used to get something out of that.\n\nBut lately… or during those times, I mostly just let those things sit. They don’t really give me anything back.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your depressed mood lasted most of the day, nearly every day, for at least 2 weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. It wasn’t just a rough weekend or something.\n\nThe low mood has been there most of the day, pretty much nearly every day, for… about a year and a couple months now, I think. It started not too long after I moved. I remember because I still had boxes stacked in the corner, and I kept telling myself I’d unpack them once I felt settled, but then weeks just kept going by.\n\nSo yeah, definitely more than two weeks. It’s been around a year and two months, give or take.\n\nDoctor: During the time you were hearing the voice or felt the cameras were tracking you, was there ever a period when those experiences were happening but you were not feeling depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a stretch like that, I think.\n\nIt wasn’t like I was cheerful exactly, but my mood had lifted some. I was getting through meals a little better, and work didn’t feel quite as impossible. I could get through my shift, do the stairwells, the hallway floors… you know, those cameras are in every hallway now, and some of them have that little red light—well, I guess not all of them do.\n\nBut even then, when I wasn’t feeling so depressed, I still heard my name in the stairwell. And I still felt sure the hallway cameras were being used to track me. So… yeah, it wasn’t only when I was really low.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling that the cameras were tracking you been going on for at least 6 months continuously?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say so. It’s been about 11 months now, the voice and that feeling with the cameras.\n\nI remember because I kept thinking it was just the building, you know, the way sound carries in stairwells. Those stairwells echo funny after they wax the floors, and the cameras—well, they’re everywhere now, even in grocery stores. But… no, it’s been going on pretty steady for around 11 months.\n\nDoctor: During that stretch when you still heard the voice and felt tracked by the cameras, were you free of depression completely?\n\nPatient: Completely free? No… I don’t think I’d say completely.\n\nThere were several weeks where my mood had lifted some. Like, I was eating a little better, and getting through work didn’t feel quite so impossible. I could do the hallways and the stairwells without dragging myself as much… though the stairwells are where I’d still hear my name sometimes.\n\nAnd the cameras, too — I still felt sure they were tracking me in the hallways, even during that better stretch. So I wasn’t as depressed then, but I wouldn’t say I was totally free of it.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time lasting at least 2 weeks when you heard the voice or felt tracked by the cameras while you were not depressed at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say not depressed at all. There were several weeks where I was… better, I guess. Like I could eat more regular, and getting through work wasn’t such a mountain. I’d do the hallways, mop around the stairwells, and I wasn’t dragging as bad.\n\nBut the voice was still there sometimes — hearing my name in the stairwell. And I still felt like the hallway cameras were tracking me. Those cameras are everywhere now, and some of them are aimed at odd angles, like not where you’d expect… but yeah, even when my mood had lifted, that part didn’t really stop.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, were you sleeping much less or much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I didn’t keep track of it like that.\n\nSome nights felt different than others, but with the cleaning work, the hours and the building routine kind of blur together. You know, you finish one floor and then another, and by the time you get home it’s hard to tell what’s “usual” anymore. So… I can’t say for sure if it was much less or much more.\n\nDoctor: At any time since the voice and the camera-tracking feeling started, were you completely free of depression for at least 2 weeks while those experiences were still happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I was completely free of it, no. Those hallways have cameras in such strange spots, you know, and when you’re cleaning at night you notice all that. So… better mood, yes, but not totally free of depression.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you feel down or depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, most of the time. Like he’s standing over by the stairwell. And I’ll turn and look, because it feels that close, but… there’s nobody there.\n\nDoctor: When the voice happens, do you feel depressed at those times?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. I mean, not every single time I hear it, maybe, but when I was really at the bottom—like barely eating, just feeling guilty about everything, even stupid things like not getting the trash liners folded right—I was hearing that man’s voice at work too.\n\nThat was also when I started feeling like people in the building were watching me. Like from the offices, or cameras maybe… I know buildings have cameras, of course, I clean around them all the time, little black domes in the corners. But it felt more personal than that. And I was having thoughts like… maybe I wouldn’t care if I didn’t wake up. So, yeah, the voice was happening when I was depressed like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard that voice during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a time like that, I think. A few weeks where I wasn’t so sunk down. I was eating a little more regular, getting through the route at work without standing there staring at the mop bucket forever.\n\nBut even then, I’d be in the stairwell and hear my name. Just my name, like someone calling from around the corner. And I still had it in my head that the hallway cameras weren’t just there for security… like they were being used to keep track of me specifically.\n\nThose cameras, they put them in those little smoked domes, you know, and you can’t tell which way they’re pointing. I used to clean under them and try not to look up too much. But yeah… that was when I wasn’t feeling as depressed as before.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least 2 weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt the cameras were tracking you, while you were not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It wasn’t just a day or two. It was, I don’t know, several weeks maybe, where I wasn’t in that deep hole like before.\n\nI was eating meals more regular, getting through work a little better… still slow, but not just standing there useless. But I’d still hear my name in the stairwell, clear enough that I’d stop and look.\n\nAnd the cameras… those hallway cameras, with the dark little domes, I still felt like they were tracking me in particular. Not just normal security. I know that sounds… but yeah, that went on while I wasn’t feeling so depressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic or high, much more than normal?\n\nPatient: Unusually energetic or high… I don’t know. I don’t think I get “high” like that, not like bouncing off the walls or anything.\n\nSometimes if I get a decent night’s sleep, I can move a little faster at work, you know, get the trash pulled before the offices start filling up. But that’s not… I mean, that’s just having a better day. The floors take forever when people track in salt in the winter, and then you’re going over the same spot again and again—\n\nSorry. I guess I’m not sure what you mean exactly. I haven’t really noticed a time where I felt way more energetic than normal.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you lose interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I did. Things just kind of went flat.\n\nI used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, even if I didn’t catch much. Just sitting there with the line out, coffee in the thermos… there’s a spot where the weeds always snag if you cast too far left, and I knew that, I liked knowing that. But during those low times I’d just let the gear sit.\n\nSame with crosswords in the morning. I used to do them with coffee, pencil all chewed up, you know. Or at work, showing the younger guys little tricks for stripping a floor so they don’t gouge it up. I used to get something out of that.\n\nBut lately… or during those times, I mostly just let those things sit. They don’t really give me anything back.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your depressed mood lasted most of the day, nearly every day, for at least 2 weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. It wasn’t just a rough weekend or something.\n\nThe low mood has been there most of the day, pretty much nearly every day, for… about a year and a couple months now, I think. It started not too long after I moved. I remember because I still had boxes stacked in the corner, and I kept telling myself I’d unpack them once I felt settled, but then weeks just kept going by.\n\nSo yeah, definitely more than two weeks. It’s been around a year and two months, give or take.\n\nDoctor: During the time you were hearing the voice or felt the cameras were tracking you, was there ever a period when those experiences were happening but you were not feeling depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a stretch like that, I think.\n\nIt wasn’t like I was cheerful exactly, but my mood had lifted some. I was getting through meals a little better, and work didn’t feel quite as impossible. I could get through my shift, do the stairwells, the hallway floors… you know, those cameras are in every hallway now, and some of them have that little red light—well, I guess not all of them do.\n\nBut even then, when I wasn’t feeling so depressed, I still heard my name in the stairwell. And I still felt sure the hallway cameras were being used to track me. So… yeah, it wasn’t only when I was really low.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling that the cameras were tracking you been going on for at least 6 months continuously?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say so. It’s been about 11 months now, the voice and that feeling with the cameras.\n\nI remember because I kept thinking it was just the building, you know, the way sound carries in stairwells. Those stairwells echo funny after they wax the floors, and the cameras—well, they’re everywhere now, even in grocery stores. But… no, it’s been going on pretty steady for around 11 months.\n\nDoctor: During that stretch when you still heard the voice and felt tracked by the cameras, were you free of depression completely?\n\nPatient: Completely free? No… I don’t think I’d say completely.\n\nThere were several weeks where my mood had lifted some. Like, I was eating a little better, and getting through work didn’t feel quite so impossible. I could do the hallways and the stairwells without dragging myself as much… though the stairwells are where I’d still hear my name sometimes.\n\nAnd the cameras, too — I still felt sure they were tracking me in the hallways, even during that better stretch. So I wasn’t as depressed then, but I wouldn’t say I was totally free of it.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time lasting at least 2 weeks when you heard the voice or felt tracked by the cameras while you were not depressed at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say not depressed at all. There were several weeks where I was… better, I guess. Like I could eat more regular, and getting through work wasn’t such a mountain. I’d do the hallways, mop around the stairwells, and I wasn’t dragging as bad.\n\nBut the voice was still there sometimes — hearing my name in the stairwell. And I still felt like the hallway cameras were tracking me. Those cameras are everywhere now, and some of them are aimed at odd angles, like not where you’d expect… but yeah, even when my mood had lifted, that part didn’t really stop.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, were you sleeping much less or much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I didn’t keep track of it like that.\n\nSome nights felt different than others, but with the cleaning work, the hours and the building routine kind of blur together. You know, you finish one floor and then another, and by the time you get home it’s hard to tell what’s “usual” anymore. So… I can’t say for sure if it was much less or much more.\n\nDoctor: At any time since the voice and the camera-tracking feeling started, were you completely free of depression for at least 2 weeks while those experiences were still happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I was completely free of it, no. There was a stretch of several weeks where I was doing better — I was eating more normal, getting through my work a little easier, not feeling so weighed down every minute.\n\nBut the voice was still happening, like hearing my name in the stairwell, and I still believed the hallway cameras were tracking me. Those hallways have cameras in such strange spots, you know, and when you’re cleaning at night you notice all that. So… better mood, yes, but not totally free of depression.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, did you feel down or depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, most of the time. Since the move, it’s like that sadness and low energy were just kind of sitting there in the background. Even on days when I was more caught up worrying about the cameras or hearing things, the down feeling was still there underneath.\n\nI’d be doing the cleaning, taking out trash, checking the same hallway lights, and it’s funny what you notice in buildings after hours… but yeah, the guilt and not really enjoying much of anything, that was there a lot. Nearly every day, I’d say, or close to it.\n\nDoctor: During those low periods, was your appetite decreased?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it was decreased. Most days food just didn’t seem like food, more like cardboard or paper. I’d get home after the shift and maybe eat a few crackers, or just skip it because it felt like too much trouble.\n\nAnd then I’d notice my work pants hanging looser, which—those pants are already cheap and the waistband never sits right, the kind they give you with those scratchy seams—but yeah, looser than they used to be. So I think I wasn’t eating much.\n\nDoctor: Before this current year-long low period began, had you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least 2 weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, sure, but whether it was most of the day, nearly every day, for two whole weeks… I don’t know. Time gets blurry when you look back like that.\n\nThere were times years ago where I felt low, but I was still going to work and doing what I had to do. Then again, work can cover things up — you mop a floor, empty bins, keep moving, and the day goes by. Some buildings have those old buzzing lights, and that kind of thing can get under your skin after a while…\n\nBut, uh, before this past year? I can’t say for sure that there was another period like that. I don’t really know.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, was there ever a time when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least 2 weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure. I know I had times before where I felt pretty low, but pinning it down to “nearly every day” and “at least two weeks” is hard for me.\n\nI remember certain jobs feeling heavier than others, especially in older buildings where the heat pipes knock and the floors never really get clean no matter how many times you go over them… but whether that was depression every day or just being worn down, I don’t know.\n\nSo before this past year, I can’t really say for sure that it happened like that.\n\nDoctor: Since this low mood started, has there ever been a period of at least 2 months when you were not depressed?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think there’s been a full two months where it lifted. It’s been pretty much with me since not long after I moved, about a year and two months now.\n\nI mean, some days are a little less heavy than others, like if the weather’s decent or the building is quiet and nobody’s tracking mud everywhere, but that’s not the same as being not depressed. It never really went away for a couple months or anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
